I don’t think so, not in a clear way. Like, I still enjoy things when I actually do them. If I watch something I like or talk to somebody I like being around, it’s not like I feel nothing or like it’s all pointless.

Yeah… I’d say it’s pretty close to constant, or at least it feels like it’s there almost every day. It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.

It’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.

Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.

Like I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.

It’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.

I guess I’m not totally sure how to separate being tired from being “down,” if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or activities you used to look forward to, no longer give you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a clear way. Like, I still enjoy things when I actually do them. If I watch something I like or talk to somebody I like being around, it’s not like I feel nothing or like it’s all pointless.\n\nI guess the only thing is sometimes I’m so worn out that I don’t have as much energy to start doing anything, so I might put stuff off or just choose something easy. But once I’m doing it, I can still get some enjoyment out of it. So I wouldn’t say the pleasure is gone exactly… it’s more that I’m tired enough that getting to the thing feels harder sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your tiredness or the feeling of being worn out is something that’s been nearly constant, even when you’re not doing much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s pretty close to constant, or at least it feels like it’s there almost every day. It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.

And then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.

I do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.

Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. If I watch something I like or talk to somebody I like being around, it’s not like I feel nothing or like it’s all pointless.\n\nI guess the only thing is sometimes I’m so worn out that I don’t have as much energy to start doing anything, so I might put stuff off or just choose something easy. But once I’m doing it, I can still get some enjoyment out of it. So I wouldn’t say the pleasure is gone exactly… it’s more that I’m tired enough that getting to the thing feels harder sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your tiredness or the feeling of being worn out is something that’s been nearly constant, even when you’re not doing much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s pretty close to constant, or at least it feels like it’s there almost every day. It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I don’t think of myself as someone who’s, like, pacing around the room all the time or unable to sit in a chair. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Yeah… yes, definitely. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. I feel worn out almost every day, even when I haven’t done anything that should make me that tired.

Like, I can get what should be a full night’s sleep, and then when I wake up my body still feels heavy and my head feels like it’s dragging. It’s not like I ran a marathon or anything — I’m just getting up to go to my cashier shift — but I already feel kind of depleted before the day even starts.

I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I still feel like this?” It’s frustrating because it makes even normal days feel like they take more out of me than they should.

Can you tell me what's been troubling you or what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… mostly I came in because I’ve just been feeling really worn out almost every day. It’s not like just being a little tired after work, you know? I can get what should be a full night’s sleep, but when I wake up for my cashier shift, my body still feels heavy and my head feels like it’s dragging behind me.\n\nIt’s been bothering me because I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I feel like I didn’t?” And then I’m starting the day already feeling run down before I even get to the register. It’s just this ongoing tiredness that I can’t seem to shake, and I figured I should probably talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most days, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t really say I’ve been feeling hopeless most days. Like, I get frustrated and kind of worn down, sure, especially when I’m tired and trying to get through the day, but I don’t think it’s the same as feeling sad all the time.\n\nAs far as interest in things, I haven’t really noticed that I don’t enjoy stuff anymore. I mean, some days I’m too drained to want to do much, but it’s not like everything feels pointless or nothing sounds good. Like, I still enjoy things when I actually do them. If I watch something I like or talk to somebody I like being around, it’s not like I feel nothing or like it’s all pointless.\n\nI guess the only thing is sometimes I’m so worn out that I don’t have as much energy to start doing anything, so I might put stuff off or just choose something easy. But once I’m doing it, I can still get some enjoyment out of it. So I wouldn’t say the pleasure is gone exactly… it’s more that I’m tired enough that getting to the thing feels harder sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your tiredness or the feeling of being worn out is something that’s been nearly constant, even when you’re not doing much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s pretty close to constant, or at least it feels like it’s there almost every day. It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I don’t think of myself as someone who’s, like, pacing around the room all the time or unable to sit in a chair. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you experience a lack of energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, definitely. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. I don’t think so, not in the sense of actually nodding off during the day. I mean, I feel tired a lot, like I’ve said, but it’s more of a worn-out feeling than that heavy sleepiness where I’m fighting to keep my eyes open.

If I’m sitting watching something, I might zone out or feel kind of drained, but I’m not usually about to fall asleep right there. Same with sitting quietly — I might feel like I don’t have much energy, but I’m still awake. So I guess I’d say no, not excessive daytime sleepiness exactly. It’s more just feeling tired and not rested.

Most nights it takes me a while, honestly. I’ll get into bed already feeling tired, like I should just be able to fall asleep, but then my mind kind of starts up.

I’ll lie there thinking about bills, or what my work schedule is going to look like, or whether things are okay at home, and it’s like I can’t get myself to fully shut down. I don’t know the exact time every night, but it’s not usually right away. It feels like I’m tired, but not settled, if that makes sense.

And even when I do fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really relaxed all the way. Like my body was in bed, but my head was still working on things.

I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I don’t think of myself as someone who’s, like, pacing around the room all the time or unable to sit in a chair. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you experience a lack of energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, definitely. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. I feel worn out almost every day, even when I haven’t done anything that should make me that tired.\n\nLike, I can get what should be a full night’s sleep, and then when I wake up my body still feels heavy and my head feels like it’s dragging. It’s not like I ran a marathon or anything — I’m just getting up to go to my cashier shift — but I already feel kind of depleted before the day even starts.\n\nAnd then at work, standing there and dealing with the register and customers, it feels harder than it probably should. Not impossible, but like I’m pushing through this tired fog. I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I still feel like this?” It’s frustrating because it makes even normal days feel like they take more out of me than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively sleepy during the day, like you could fall asleep while sitting quietly or watching something, even after getting enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of actually nodding off during the day. I mean, I feel tired a lot, like I’ve said, but it’s more of a worn-out feeling than that heavy sleepiness where I’m fighting to keep my eyes open.\n\nIf I’m sitting watching something, I might zone out or feel kind of drained, but I’m not usually about to fall asleep right there. Same with sitting quietly — I might feel like I don’t have much energy, but I’m still awake. So I guess I’d say no, not excessive daytime sleepiness exactly. It’s more just feeling tired and not rested.\n\nDoctor: How long does it typically take you to fall asleep once you get into bed?\n\nPatient: Most nights it takes me a while, honestly. I’ll get into bed already feeling tired, like I should just be able to fall asleep, but then my mind kind of starts up.\n\nI’ll lie there thinking about bills, or what my work schedule is going to look like, or whether things are okay at home, and it’s like I can’t get myself to fully shut down. I don’t know the exact time every night, but it’s not usually right away. It feels like I’m tired, but not settled, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd even when I do fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really relaxed all the way. Like my body was in bed, but my head was still working on things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not totally sure, honestly. I haven’t really paid close attention to my muscles specifically, if that makes sense. Like, when I’m lying down trying to sleep, I’m aware of not feeling fully relaxed, but I don’t know if I’d describe it as my muscles being tight or tense exactly.

Maybe sometimes I’m kind of stiff without realizing it, but it’s not something where I’m like, “Oh, my shoulders are clenched,” or “my jaw is tight,” at least not that I’ve noticed in a clear way. I think I mostly notice that I’m not settling down, more than noticing a specific muscle tension thing.

So… I guess I’d say I don’t really know. It might be happening a little, but it’s not something I’ve been aware of enough to say yes for sure.

It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, everybody gets annoyed sometimes, so it’s hard for me to tell if it’s more than normal or if I’m just noticing it because I’ve been thinking about all this stuff more.

When I’m tired or have a lot going on, I probably have less patience, like I might get quieter or feel bothered by small things, but I don’t know that I’d say I’ve been snapping at people or getting really irritable in a way that stands out. It’s not something I’ve been keeping track of, and nobody’s really told me, “Hey, you’ve been angry lately,” or anything like that.

So… I guess I’d say maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure it’s enough for me to confidently say yes. It’s more like I feel worn down, and then things can get to me easier, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big change.

Like, I get frustrated and kind of worn down, sure, especially when I’m tired and trying to get through the day, but I don’t think it’s the same as feeling sad all the time.\n\nAs far as interest in things, I haven’t really noticed that I don’t enjoy stuff anymore. I mean, some days I’m too drained to want to do much, but it’s not like everything feels pointless or nothing sounds good. If I watch something I like or talk to somebody I like being around, it’s not like I feel nothing or like it’s all pointless.\n\nI guess the only thing is sometimes I’m so worn out that I don’t have as much energy to start doing anything, so I might put stuff off or just choose something easy. But once I’m doing it, I can still get some enjoyment out of it. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you experience a lack of energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, definitely. Not impossible, but like I’m pushing through this tired fog. I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I still feel like this?” It’s frustrating because it makes even normal days feel like they take more out of me than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively sleepy during the day, like you could fall asleep while sitting quietly or watching something, even after getting enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of actually nodding off during the day. I don’t know that I’ve really noticed a clear pattern with that. Like, I do get random aches or stomach stuff once in a while, but I’ve never really connected it in my head to being stressed or worried, if that makes sense.

It’s just this ongoing tiredness that I can’t seem to shake, and I figured I should probably talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or hopeless most days, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t really say I’ve been feeling hopeless most days. Like, I get frustrated and kind of worn down, sure, especially when I’m tired and trying to get through the day, but I don’t think it’s the same as feeling sad all the time.\n\nAs far as interest in things, I haven’t really noticed that I don’t enjoy stuff anymore. I mean, some days I’m too drained to want to do much, but it’s not like everything feels pointless or nothing sounds good. I guess I’m not totally sure how to separate being tired from being “down,” if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, time with friends, or activities you used to look forward to, no longer give you the same sense of pleasure or satisfaction?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a clear way. Like, I still enjoy things when I actually do them. If I watch something I like or talk to somebody I like being around, it’s not like I feel nothing or like it’s all pointless.\n\nI guess the only thing is sometimes I’m so worn out that I don’t have as much energy to start doing anything, so I might put stuff off or just choose something easy. But once I’m doing it, I can still get some enjoyment out of it. So I wouldn’t say the pleasure is gone exactly… it’s more that I’m tired enough that getting to the thing feels harder sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your tiredness or the feeling of being worn out is something that’s been nearly constant, even when you’re not doing much physically or mentally?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s pretty close to constant, or at least it feels like it’s there almost every day. It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I don’t think of myself as someone who’s, like, pacing around the room all the time or unable to sit in a chair. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you experience a lack of energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, definitely. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. I feel worn out almost every day, even when I haven’t done anything that should make me that tired.\n\nLike, I can get what should be a full night’s sleep, and then when I wake up my body still feels heavy and my head feels like it’s dragging. It’s not like I ran a marathon or anything — I’m just getting up to go to my cashier shift — but I already feel kind of depleted before the day even starts.\n\nAnd then at work, standing there and dealing with the register and customers, it feels harder than it probably should. Not impossible, but like I’m pushing through this tired fog. I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I still feel like this?” It’s frustrating because it makes even normal days feel like they take more out of me than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively sleepy during the day, like you could fall asleep while sitting quietly or watching something, even after getting enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of actually nodding off during the day. I mean, I feel tired a lot, like I’ve said, but it’s more of a worn-out feeling than that heavy sleepiness where I’m fighting to keep my eyes open.\n\nIf I’m sitting watching something, I might zone out or feel kind of drained, but I’m not usually about to fall asleep right there. Same with sitting quietly — I might feel like I don’t have much energy, but I’m still awake. So I guess I’d say no, not excessive daytime sleepiness exactly. It’s more just feeling tired and not rested.\n\nDoctor: How long does it typically take you to fall asleep once you get into bed?\n\nPatient: Most nights it takes me a while, honestly. I’ll get into bed already feeling tired, like I should just be able to fall asleep, but then my mind kind of starts up.\n\nI’ll lie there thinking about bills, or what my work schedule is going to look like, or whether things are okay at home, and it’s like I can’t get myself to fully shut down. I don’t know the exact time every night, but it’s not usually right away. It feels like I’m tired, but not settled, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd even when I do fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really relaxed all the way. Like my body was in bed, but my head was still working on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice that your muscles feel tense or tight, especially when you're trying to relax or fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I haven’t really paid close attention to my muscles specifically, if that makes sense. Like, when I’m lying down trying to sleep, I’m aware of not feeling fully relaxed, but I don’t know if I’d describe it as my muscles being tight or tense exactly.\n\nMaybe sometimes I’m kind of stiff without realizing it, but it’s not something where I’m like, “Oh, my shoulders are clenched,” or “my jaw is tight,” at least not that I’ve noticed in a clear way. I think I mostly notice that I’m not settling down, more than noticing a specific muscle tension thing.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I don’t really know. It might be happening a little, but it’s not something I’ve been aware of enough to say yes for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself getting more easily annoyed or irritated lately, especially when you're feeling tired or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. It’s not something I’ve been keeping track of, and nobody’s really told me, “Hey, you’ve been angry lately,” or anything like that.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure it’s enough for me to confidently say yes. Yeah… that one I do notice more. A lot of nights I’ll get into bed feeling like I should be able to just pass out, because I’m tired from the day, but then once I’m actually lying there it’s like my brain starts going through everything.

It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I don’t think of myself as someone who’s, like, pacing around the room all the time or unable to sit in a chair. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you experience a lack of energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, definitely. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. I feel worn out almost every day, even when I haven’t done anything that should make me that tired.\n\nLike, I can get what should be a full night’s sleep, and then when I wake up my body still feels heavy and my head feels like it’s dragging. It’s not like I ran a marathon or anything — I’m just getting up to go to my cashier shift — but I already feel kind of depleted before the day even starts.\n\nAnd then at work, standing there and dealing with the register and customers, it feels harder than it probably should. Not impossible, but like I’m pushing through this tired fog. I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I still feel like this?” It’s frustrating because it makes even normal days feel like they take more out of me than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively sleepy during the day, like you could fall asleep while sitting quietly or watching something, even after getting enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of actually nodding off during the day. I mean, I feel tired a lot, like I’ve said, but it’s more of a worn-out feeling than that heavy sleepiness where I’m fighting to keep my eyes open.\n\nIf I’m sitting watching something, I might zone out or feel kind of drained, but I’m not usually about to fall asleep right there. Same with sitting quietly — I might feel like I don’t have much energy, but I’m still awake. So I guess I’d say no, not excessive daytime sleepiness exactly. It’s more just feeling tired and not rested.\n\nDoctor: How long does it typically take you to fall asleep once you get into bed?\n\nPatient: Most nights it takes me a while, honestly. I’ll get into bed already feeling tired, like I should just be able to fall asleep, but then my mind kind of starts up.\n\nI’ll lie there thinking about bills, or what my work schedule is going to look like, or whether things are okay at home, and it’s like I can’t get myself to fully shut down. I don’t know the exact time every night, but it’s not usually right away. It feels like I’m tired, but not settled, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd even when I do fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really relaxed all the way. Like my body was in bed, but my head was still working on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice that your muscles feel tense or tight, especially when you're trying to relax or fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I haven’t really paid close attention to my muscles specifically, if that makes sense. Like, when I’m lying down trying to sleep, I’m aware of not feeling fully relaxed, but I don’t know if I’d describe it as my muscles being tight or tense exactly.\n\nMaybe sometimes I’m kind of stiff without realizing it, but it’s not something where I’m like, “Oh, my shoulders are clenched,” or “my jaw is tight,” at least not that I’ve noticed in a clear way. I think I mostly notice that I’m not settling down, more than noticing a specific muscle tension thing.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I don’t really know. It might be happening a little, but it’s not something I’ve been aware of enough to say yes for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself getting more easily annoyed or irritated lately, especially when you're feeling tired or overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, everybody gets annoyed sometimes, so it’s hard for me to tell if it’s more than normal or if I’m just noticing it because I’ve been thinking about all this stuff more.\n\nWhen I’m tired or have a lot going on, I probably have less patience, like I might get quieter or feel bothered by small things, but I don’t know that I’d say I’ve been snapping at people or getting really irritable in a way that stands out. It’s not something I’ve been keeping track of, and nobody’s really told me, “Hey, you’ve been angry lately,” or anything like that.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say maybe a little sometimes, but I’m not sure it’s enough for me to confidently say yes. It’s more like I feel worn down, and then things can get to me easier, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big change.\n\nDoctor: Do you often notice physical symptoms like stomach discomfort, headaches, or aches in your body that seem to come up when you're feeling stressed or worried?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve really noticed a clear pattern with that. Like, I do get random aches or stomach stuff once in a while, but I’ve never really connected it in my head to being stressed or worried, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I’m stressed, I mostly notice that I’m kind of caught up in my thoughts and can’t settle, more than noticing, like, “Oh, now my stomach hurts because I’m worried.” I don’t usually track it that closely. Sometimes after a long shift standing at work, my body might feel sore, but that feels more like just being on my feet than anything emotional.\n\nHeadaches… maybe here and there, but not in a way where I’d say it happens often or definitely because I’m stressed. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. It’s possible I don’t pay enough attention to it, but nothing really stands out to me as a regular physical symptom that comes up when I’m worried.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you haven't rested well during the night, even if you slept for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one I do notice more. A lot of nights I’ll get into bed feeling like I should be able to just pass out, because I’m tired from the day, but then once I’m actually lying there it’s like my brain starts going through everything.\n\nI’ll think about bills, or whether I’m going to have enough to cover what’s coming up, and then I start thinking about my work schedule, like if I’m forgetting a shift or if something’s going to get messed up. And then I’ll think about people at home, whether everybody’s okay, if there’s something I should’ve done or checked on. It’s not always one big thing, it’s more like a bunch of little worries lining up one after another.\n\nAnd even if I technically sleep for a decent amount of time, I don’t always wake up feeling rested. It feels more like I was never fully settled, if that makes sense. Like my body was asleep but my mind didn’t really shut off all the way. So yeah, most nights it kind of feels like I didn’t get good rest, even if I was in bed long enough.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s more like I feel worn down, and then things can get to me easier, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big change.\n\nDoctor: Do you often notice physical symptoms like stomach discomfort, headaches, or aches in your body that seem to come up when you're feeling stressed or worried?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve really noticed a clear pattern with that. Like, I do get random aches or stomach stuff once in a while, but I’ve never really connected it in my head to being stressed or worried, if that makes sense.\n\nWhen I’m stressed, I mostly notice that I’m kind of caught up in my thoughts and can’t settle, more than noticing, like, “Oh, now my stomach hurts because I’m worried.” I don’t usually track it that closely. Sometimes after a long shift standing at work, my body might feel sore, but that feels more like just being on my feet than anything emotional.\n\nHeadaches… maybe here and there, but not in a way where I’d say it happens often or definitely because I’m stressed. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. It’s possible I don’t pay enough attention to it, but nothing really stands out to me as a regular physical symptom that comes up when I’m worried.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you haven't rested well during the night, even if you slept for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one I do notice more. A lot of nights I’ll get into bed feeling like I should be able to just pass out, because I’m tired from the day, but then once I’m actually lying there it’s like my brain starts going through everything.\n\nI’ll think about bills, or whether I’m going to have enough to cover what’s coming up, and then I start thinking about my work schedule, like if I’m forgetting a shift or if something’s going to get messed up. And then I’ll think about people at home, whether everybody’s okay, if there’s something I should’ve done or checked on. It’s not always one big thing, it’s more like a bunch of little worries lining up one after another.\n\nAnd even if I technically sleep for a decent amount of time, I don’t always wake up feeling rested. It feels more like I was never fully settled, if that makes sense. Like my body was asleep but my mind didn’t really shut off all the way. So yeah, most nights it kind of feels like I didn’t get good rest, even if I was in bed long enough.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever notice that your muscles feel tense or tight, especially when you're trying to relax or fall asleep?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not totally sure. I mean, I know what you mean by tense, like shoulders up or jaw clenched or feeling stiff, but I don’t think I’ve really noticed that as a regular thing, especially not in a way where I’m lying there thinking, “My muscles are tight.”

But once I’m doing it, I can still get some enjoyment out of it. It’s not only after I’ve been running around or doing something hard. Even on mornings where I’ve slept what should be enough hours, I get up for my cashier shift and my body just feels heavy, like I’m already used up before the day starts.\n\nAnd my head feels kind of the same way, like it’s dragging behind me. I’ll be getting ready or just standing there before work, not doing anything major, and I still feel worn out. That’s part of why it started bothering me so much, because I keep thinking, “I slept, I didn’t do anything yet, so why do I feel this tired already?”\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sleep is restful, or do you wake up still feeling unrefreshed even after a full night’s sleep?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’ll go to bed tired—like genuinely tired—and I’ll think, okay, I should just fall asleep because I’m worn out. But then once I’m actually in bed, my mind kind of turns on. I start going over bills, whether I missed something with my work schedule, what time I have to be up, and then I’ll think about people at home and whether everyone’s okay, that kind of thing.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to think about all of it. It just sort of starts looping, and I’ll be lying there feeling tired but not really settled. And even when I do sleep through enough hours, I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Like my body was asleep, maybe, but my head never fully shut off. So yeah, I’d say a full night doesn’t always feel like a full night.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, or feeling anxious in a way that’s hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably the biggest thing, honestly. It’s not always one huge problem right in front of me, it’s more like my mind keeps grabbing onto the next thing and then the next thing after that.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about money, then I’ll switch to worrying about the relative staying with us and whether that’s causing strain, then I’m thinking about my wife being stressed, and then I’ll remember something from church I said I’d help with or should be keeping up with. And then somehow it circles back to work, like, “Did I mess something up on the register?” or “Did I come across wrong to somebody?” even if nobody said I did.\n\nIt’s hard to shut it off because each thing feels like it could become a real problem if I don’t keep thinking about it, if that makes sense. But thinking about it doesn’t really solve anything most of the time. It just keeps moving around in my head. So yeah, I do worry more than I think I should, and it’s hard to control once it gets going.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or do you wake up frequently during the night?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly it’s trouble falling asleep. I’ll be really tired when I get into bed, like my body is ready to be done for the day, but then as soon as I’m lying there my mind starts going. I’ll start thinking about bills, whether I’m going to be short on something, then I’ll think about my work schedule and whether I remembered it right, or if I need to be up earlier than I thought.\n\nAnd then it kind of branches out from there, like I’ll start wondering if everyone at home is okay or if there’s something I should’ve handled during the day. It’s not always one specific thing, it’s just this running list that feels like it won’t stop. So I’m in bed tired, but not relaxed, if that makes sense.\n\nI do sleep eventually, but even then it doesn’t feel like I really settled down. I’ll wake up and feel like I was never fully at rest, like I slept but didn’t actually get restored by it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel physically restless, like you can’t sit still or relax your body, even when you’re not actively doing anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that one. I don’t think of myself as someone who’s, like, pacing around the room all the time or unable to sit in a chair. I mean, if I’m waiting somewhere or I’m sitting after work, I can usually sit there.\n\nMaybe I shift around some or tap my foot without really noticing, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean or if that’s just normal. I wouldn’t say it’s the main thing I notice in my body. It’s more like… I can be sitting still on the outside, but I’m not sure I’m fully relaxed, if that makes sense. But physically restless, like I can’t sit still, I don’t know that I’d say that’s a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you experience a lack of energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, definitely. That’s actually a big part of why I came in. It’s not like I ran a marathon or anything — I’m just getting up to go to my cashier shift — but I already feel kind of depleted before the day even starts.\n\nAnd then at work, standing there and dealing with the register and customers, it feels harder than it probably should. Not impossible, but like I’m pushing through this tired fog. I keep thinking, “I slept, so why do I still feel like this?” It’s frustrating because it makes even normal days feel like they take more out of me than they should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel excessively sleepy during the day, like you could fall asleep while sitting quietly or watching something, even after getting enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the sense of actually nodding off during the day. I mean, I feel tired a lot, like I’ve said, but it’s more of a worn-out feeling than that heavy sleepiness where I’m fighting to keep my eyes open.\n\nIf I’m sitting watching something, I might zone out or feel kind of drained, but I’m not usually about to fall asleep right there. Same with sitting quietly — I might feel like I don’t have much energy, but I’m still awake. So I guess I’d say no, not excessive daytime sleepiness exactly. It’s more just feeling tired and not rested.\n\nDoctor: How long does it typically take you to fall asleep once you get into bed?\n\nPatient: Most nights it takes me a while, honestly. I’ll get into bed already feeling tired, like I should just be able to fall asleep, but then my mind kind of starts up.\n\nI’ll lie there thinking about bills, or what my work schedule is going to look like, or whether things are okay at home, and it’s like I can’t get myself to fully shut down. I don’t know the exact time every night, but it’s not usually right away. It feels like I’m tired, but not settled, if that makes sense.\n\nAnd even when I do fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I never really relaxed all the way.
